A Chinese man in his early thirties, his body stretched to an elongated 1.5 times normal height, stands stiffly between two women. His springy halo of black curls frames a face with a compressed, anxious line of a mouth and visibly flared nostrils. He wears a crisp white silk shirt and matching trousers, with a long scarf in coral and ultramarine knotted loosely around his neck. On his left, a Chinese woman in her late twenties, identified as Woman A, uses her dramatically elongated arms—each 2.3 times their normal length—to envelop his shoulders. One of her oversized hands cups his chin, physically turning his head to face her. Her fan-shaped braid of vibrant copper hair is immaculate, and her face, with its triangular, shouting mouth and asymmetrically raised brows, beams a performatively bright smile. Her coral satin trench coat is flung open, revealing a matching lace dress beneath, cinched with a wide ultramarine belt. On the man's right, the other Chinese woman, Woman B, in her early thirties, anchors herself against him. Her upper torso is widened to 1.8 times normal width, a solid block of ultramarine coarse-knit fabric from her severe, swept-back crest of silver hair down to her hips. Her expression is a smug crescent smile, cheeks tightened with possessive satisfaction. His face, with tightened cheek muscles and a compressed line of a mouth, is caught in a silent grimace of physical strain. To his left stands a woman in her early thirties, her severe swept-back crest of burgundy hair sharp against the light. Her exaggeratedly long arms, elongated to 2.4 times their normal length, are entirely wrapped around him: one forearm pulls his waist to the left with a visible grip, the other snakes up his back and around his upper arm, yanking his shoulder laterally. Her body is encased in a structured burgundy corset of matte wool, its electric blue piping tracing severe lines, with matching trousers. Her face, tilted up towards him, is a portrait of determined effort, with a widened megaphone mouth shouting a silent demand and nostrils flared, her electric-blue-painted lips stark against the burgundy wool directly below them, where the bold electric blue screen-printed words “PULL LEFT” are emblazoned across the corset’s front panel. To the man’s right is the second woman, also in her early thirties, whose sculpted mass of electric blue silk waves frames a head enlarged to 1.7 times its normal size. She presses her enlarged cheek intimately against his shoulder, her smug crescent mouth curved in a knowing smile and her narrowed eyes fixed on her rival. Her glossy electric blue silk wrap dress is partially open, revealing her bare torso and the curve of one breast, held together by a burgundy-embroidered sash that cinches below her chest. Her hands rest possessively on the man’s hip, one thumb hooking his belt loop, anchoring him with adhesive pressure. On the vibrant electric blue silk of her sash, the words “STICK RIGHT” are screen-printed in contrasting burgundy. The environment is a minimalist Chinese apartment entryway, dominated by a single deep burgundy console table against a smooth, shadowed wall. The composition is a forceful diagonal, the man’s stretched body forming the central axis being torqued in opposite directions by the two women. Hard theatrical side light from an overhead source creates sharp, elongated shadows and highlights the textures: the matte wool of the corset, the glossy silk of the dress, and the stretched, strained weave of the man’s linen shirt, which pulls taut across his twisted torso. The candy-coated hostility is palpable in their exaggerated, contrasting expressions and the visible physical stress on the man’s frame, all rendered in hyper-realistic detail with impossible, caricatural proportions.
